--- Commit Message ---
LU-18158 sec: retry mechanism for most syscalls

In addition to file/dir create, a number of other syscalls can
benefit from a retry mechanism in case of -EACCES. This can happen if
the client was not able to send the correct supplementary groups.
After the first failed attempt, the failure reply from the
server includes a hint with a possible supplementary group and ACLs.
The client uses this hint from the reply and retries with alternative
supplementary groups.

The syscalls handled by this patch are:
- open
- get/setattr
- mkdir
- unlink
- rename
- link

rename is special because unlike other syscalls handled by this patch,
access control is carried out based on permissions of source file,
source directory and target directory. As we do not have enough room
in the MDS_REINT_RENAME request, we proceed in 2 steps. If the rename
operation initially fails with -EACCES, the server hints the client
with the ACLs of the source directory, and the client tries again the
rename with this additional information. If it fails again with
-EACCES, the clients sets the MDS_RENAME_AGAIN op bias, so that the
server knows it needs to hint with the target dir ACLs this time in
the reply. The client then tries one last time the rename with this
additional information.

For the reply to have room for the ACL, RMF_ACL is added to the
MDS_REINT_UNLINK, MDS_REINT_LINK, MDS_REINT_RENAME and
MDS_REINT_CREATE_ACL reply formats, and to MDS_REINT_CREATE_SYM so
that it keeps matching MDS_REINT_CREATE_ACL, which is the format the
client uses to send a symlink create. The client and the server both
set the ACL reply buffer size to 0 as long as the hint is not needed.

sanity-sec test_80 is added to exercise this.

Test-Parameters: serverversion=2.15 testlist=sanity env=EXCEPT="24I 24v 27cg 51b 160a 230k 230w 271f 272 277 421d 807"

Fixes: e603ddadd5 ("LU-18158 sec: hint client in case of failed reint open")
Fixes: e826e8bb77 ("LU-17961 sec: support supplementary groups from client")

## Summary

**Fix Applied:**

I replaced all three occurrences of the incorrect function name `ptlrpc_req_finished()` with the correct function name `ptlrpc_req_put()` in `lustre/llite/namei.c` at:
- Line 1760 (in `__ll_create` function)
- Line 2345 (in `__ll_unlink` function)  
- Line 2546 (in `__ll_rename` function)

**Rationale:**

The compile error occurred because the patch introduced calls to `ptlrpc_req_finished()`, which does not exist in the Lustre codebase. By examining the existing code in `namei.c` and the Lustre headers, I found that the correct function for releasing/freeing ptlrpc requests is `ptlrpc_req_put()`, which is declared in `lustre/include/lustre_net.h`. 

All other request cleanup code in the same file consistently uses `ptlrpc_req_put()`, so this was clearly a typo in the patch where the author used the wrong function name. The fix ensures consistency with the rest of the codebase and resolves the implicit function declaration error.

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-07-28 06:54:13.000000000) ---
Patch Set 15:

(12 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments. The biggest one is that the second supplementary group is now taken from the wire for REINT_CREATE/REINT_UNLINK without going through nodemap_map_suppgid(), unlike link/rename/open. sanity-sec test_80 only exercises the case where the caller is *not* a member of the target's group (so op_suppgids[0] is INVALID_GID and the hint lands in slot 0); a case where the caller already has a valid op_suppgids[0] and the useful group only appears in the ACL would be worth adding, since that is where the setattr retry does not work.
